    fn validate_action_sequence(
        &self,
        f: &Function,
        seq: &[Instruction],
        s: &mut Discipline,
    ) -> Result<()> {
        for i in seq {
            let op = i.op.as_str();
            let a = &i.operands;
            let arity = |n: usize, lhs: usize| -> Result<()> {
                if a.len() != n || i.lhs.len() != lhs {
                    err("§4.8", format!("{op}: wrong arity at line {}", i.line))
                } else {
                    Ok(())
                }
            };
            let scalar = |s: &Discipline, n: &str| -> Result<()> {
                if s.wires(n)?.len() != 1 {
                    err("§3.3", format!("{n} must name one wire"))
                } else {
                    Ok(())
                }
            };
            if !matches!(op, "qif" | "qloop") && !i.bodies.is_empty() {
                return err("§4.8", "opcode does not take a structured body");
            }
            match op {
                "qif" => {
                    arity(1, 0)?;
                    scalar(s, &a[0])?;
                    if i.bodies.len() != 2 {
                        return err("§4.8", "qif needs two arms");
                    }
                    let outer = s.predicates.clone();
                    let outer_floor = s.predicate_floor;
                    for (arm, body) in i.bodies.iter().enumerate() {
                        let mut expected = outer.clone();
                        expected.push(if arm == 0 {
                            a[0].clone()
                        } else {
                            format!("!{}", a[0])
                        });
        _ => return None,
    };
    Some(format!("{inverse}:{rest}"))
}
pub(crate) fn gate(op: &str) -> bool {
    [
        "x", "h", "s", "sdg", "t", "tdg", "cx", "ccx", "mcx", "swap", "cz", "rx", "ry", "rz",
    ]
    .contains(&op)
}
pub(crate) fn gate_operands(i: &Instruction) -> Result<(Vec<String>, Vec<String>, bool, bool)> {
    let a = &i.operands;
    let op = i.op.as_str();
    let n = match op {
        "x" | "h" | "s" | "sdg" | "t" | "tdg" => 1,
        "ccx" => 3,
        _ => 2,
    };
    if !i.lhs.is_empty() || a.len() != n {
        return err("§4.8", "gate arity mismatch");
    }
    let diagonal = matches!(op, "s" | "sdg" | "t" | "tdg" | "cz" | "rz");
    let other = matches!(op, "h" | "rx" | "ry");
    let (controls, targets) = match op {
        "cx" => (vec![a[0].clone()], vec![a[1].clone()]),
        "ccx" => (a[..2].to_vec(), vec![a[2].clone()]),
        "mcx" => (list(&a[0])?, vec![a[1].clone()]),
        "swap" | "cz" => (Vec::new(), a.clone()),
        "rx" | "ry" | "rz" => {
            if let Ok(x) = a[0].parse::<f64>() {
                if !x.is_finite() || !a[0].contains(['.', 'e', 'E']) {
                    return err("§16.1", "angle must be finite FLOAT");
                }
            } else if !name(&a[0]) {
                return err("§4.4", "angle must be literal or classical argument");
            }
            (Vec::new(), vec![a[1].clone()])
        }
        _ => (Vec::new(), vec![a[0].clone()]),
    };
    Ok((controls, targets, diagonal, other))
}
pub(crate) struct NormalCall {
    pub instruction: Instruction,
    pub control: Option<String>,
    pub adjoint: bool,
}
pub(crate) fn normalize_call(i: &Instruction) -> Result<NormalCall> {
    let mut out = i.clone();
    let mut control = None;
    let mut adjoint = false;
    loop {
        match out.op.as_str() {
            "adj" => {
                adjoint = !adjoint;
                if out.operands.is_empty() {
                    return err("§4.7", "missing adjoint call");
                }
                out.op = out.operands.remove(0);
            }
            "ctl" => {
                if control.is_some() || out.operands.len() < 2 {
                    return err("§4.7", "invalid controlled call");
                }
                control = Some(out.operands.remove(0));
                out.op = out.operands.remove(0);
            }
            _ => break,
        }
    }
    if !matches!(out.op.as_str(), "oracle_call" | "action_call") {
        return err("§4.7", "adj/ctl requires a region call");
    }
    Ok(NormalCall {
        instruction: out,
        control,
        adjoint,
    })
}

/// Lexical declarations, including zero-trip loops and both qif arms.
    }
    fn action_sequence(
        &self,
        sequence: &[Instruction],
        widths: &[LifetimeWidths],
        q: &mut QuantumState,
        e: &mut Environment,
        adjoint: bool,
        inherited: &[(Wire, bool)],
    ) -> Result<()> {
        let indices: Vec<_> = if adjoint {
            (0..sequence.len()).rev().collect()
        } else {
            (0..sequence.len()).collect()
        };
        for index in indices {
            let i = &sequence[index];
            let width = &widths[index];
            let a = &i.operands;
            let context = e.controls(inherited);
            let op = i.op.as_str();
            if gate(op) {
                if !matches!(op, "x" | "cx" | "ccx" | "mcx" | "swap") {
                    q.audit_frame()?;
                }
                let mut wires = Vec::new();
                let mut angle = None;
                if op == "mcx" {
                    for n in list(&a[0])? {
                        wires.push(e.scalar(&n)?);
                    }
                    wires.push(e.scalar(&a[1])?);
                } else if matches!(op, "rx" | "ry" | "rz") {
                    let theta = match a[0].parse::<f64>() {
                        Ok(n) => n,
                        Err(_) => match e.bindings.args.get(&a[0]) {
                            Some(Argument::Float(n)) => *n,
                            _ => return err("§4.4", "angle is not a finite classical f64"),
                        },
                    };
                    angle = Some(if adjoint { -theta } else { theta });
                    wires.push(e.scalar(&a[1])?);
                } else {
                    for n in a {
                        wires.push(e.scalar(n)?);
                    }
                }
                let inverse = if adjoint {
                    match op {
                        "s" => "sdg",
                        "sdg" => "s",
                        "t" => "tdg",
                        "tdg" => "t",
                        _ => op,
                    }
                } else {
                    op
                };
                q.controlled_gate(inverse, &wires, angle, &context)?;
                continue;
                    out.extend(self.call(&call, env)?);
                    out.extend([
                        obj([("relinquish", text(&t))]),
                        obj([("relinquish", text(&f))]),
                    ]);
                    env.wires.remove(&t);
                    env.wires.remove(&f);
                }
                "oracle_call" | "action_call" | "ctl" | "adj" => out.extend(self.call(i, env)?),
                _ => {
                    let mut node = if matches!(op, "rx" | "ry" | "rz") {
                        let angle = if let Some(value) = self.values.get(&a[0]) {
                            value.clone()
                        } else {
                            text(format!(
                                "0x{:016x}",
                                a[0].parse::<f64>()
                                    .map_err(|_| Error::new("§11.1: malformed angle".into()))?
                                    .to_bits()
                            ))
                        };
                        obj([
                            ("gate", text(op)),
                            ("wires", array([env.wire(&a[1])?])),
                            ("params", array([angle])),
                        ])
                    } else if op == "mcx" {
                        let mut wires = crate::object::list(&a[0])?
                            .iter()
                            .map(|n| env.wire(n))
                            .collect::<Result<Vec<_>>>()?;
                        wires.push(env.wire(&a[1])?);
                        gate(op, wires)
                    } else if matches!(
                        op,
                        "x" | "h" | "s" | "sdg" | "t" | "tdg" | "cx" | "cz" | "swap" | "ccx"
                    ) {
                        gate(
                            op,
                            a.iter().map(|n| env.wire(n)).collect::<Result<Vec<_>>>()?,
                        )
                    } else {
                        return Err(Error::new(format!(
                            "§11.1: unknown source action operation {op}"
                        )));
                    };
                    // Keep the JSON node's typed angle rather than host float text.
                    out.extend(controlled(
                        env,
                        vec![std::mem::replace(&mut node, Value::Null)],
                    ));
                }
            }
"""CLI: python3 -I -m checker_replay CERT --object OBJ [--target T] [--schedule S] [--max-elements N]
       python3 -I -m checker_replay --from-object OBJ [--target T] [--schedule S]

One canonical JSON verdict on stdout. Exit: 0 every claim replayed; 1 a rejection;
2 something unchecked, no rejection; 3 usage or input error.
"""
import argparse, json, sys
from . import jcs
from .objparse import parse_object
from .replay import check_certificate, DEFAULT_MAX_ELEMENTS


def _no_dup(pairs):
    d = {}
    for k, v in pairs:
        if k in d:
            raise ValueError(f"duplicate JSON key {k!r}")
        d[k] = v
    return d


def _load(path):
    with open(path, encoding='utf-8') as f:
        return json.load(f, object_pairs_hook=_no_dup)


def main(argv=None):
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(prog="checker_replay", exit_on_error=False)
    ap.add_argument("certificate", nargs="?")
    ap.add_argument("--object")
    ap.add_argument("--from-object")
    ap.add_argument("--target")
    ap.add_argument("--schedule")
    ap.add_argument("--max-elements", type=int, default=DEFAULT_MAX_ELEMENTS)
    try:
        a = ap.parse_args(argv)
        path = a.from_object or a.object
        if path is None or (a.from_object and a.certificate) or not (a.from_object or a.certificate):
            raise ValueError("give CERT --object OBJ, or --from-object OBJ")
        with open(path, encoding='utf-8', newline='') as f:
            text = f.read()
        obj = parse_object(text)
        cert = obj.certificate if a.from_object else _load(a.certificate)
        if cert is None:
            raise ValueError(f"{path} embeds no certificate")
        target = _load(a.target) if a.target else None
        schedule = _load(a.schedule) if a.schedule else None
    except (OSError, ValueError, argparse.ArgumentError) as e:
        print(f"checker_replay: {e}", file=sys.stderr)
        return 3
    v = check_certificate(cert, obj, text, target=target, schedule=schedule, max_elements=a.max_elements)
    sys.stdout.write(jcs.canonical(v).decode() + "\n")
    return {"reject": 1, "unchecked": 2, "replayed": 0}[v["overall"]]


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())

# Mixed fat object — shared contract scope

Proposal for every revision in this directory; not a published ISA amendment.
Clause references are to [ISA 0.9](../../../spec/Bennett-ISA-Spec-0.9.md),
[the North Star](../../../../bennett/docs/Bennett-NorthStar.md), and, where
qualified, the maintained dialect 0.2/0.3 texts. This is the shared statement
required by bennett-4ht and the council rubric's standing mixed-fat-object check.

## Citable scoping statement

> A mixed fat object retains an ordinary native host binary and embeds ISA
> objects for marked device modules and their linked device closures (North
> Star §§2, 3.3, 4.4). ISA §§9.1–9.3 identify each device object and selected
> root; §12.3 certificates, owner records and reports cover that closure,
> with a separate binding/context set per device root and [] relative to
> that root (§12.1). Native entry, argv/envp and host-only libc need no ISA
> region, frame or certificate record; native comparison binds the selected
> materialised closure, not the whole host. Today §10 launches a device CC
> entry with copied arguments/entropy; §5.4 budgets bind device invocations,
> §3.3/§10 handles belong to a loaded program/run, and §7.3/§10 tapes cover
> device R effects. These require explicit container-to-object/root/certificate
> association, host-entry-to-device-entry bindings, budget/handle lifetime
> negotiation and tape/run association in a versioned mixed-launch contract.
> ISA §7.1 defines device memory but §10 defines no general host-memory-by-reference
> launch ABI: that contract must add address/layout mapping, range/liveness,
> aliasing, ownership/synchronisation, writeback and reversal rules, preserving
> dialect §§6.6, 8.5, 19.4–19.5. Until published, these are explicit
> bennett-4ht boundary obligations, not grounds to require whole-binary
> lowering or omit mixed execution.

## Present assumptions and exact publication changes

| Contract object | Assumption today | Exact change needed for mixed use |
|---|---|---|
| Object and linking (§§9.1–9.3) | One ISA object's closed sections and linked symbols; no native-container schema. | Publish a versioned inventory associating embedded/linked ISA object bytes and object_hash with selected device roots and certificates. A native-container hash, if used, is a separate identity. |
| Certificates/inventory (§§12.1–12.3) | Closure-keyed device-region records; current text does not explicitly delimit native inventory. | Insert the shared scoping rule in §12.3, cross-reference from §9.2, and require complete device-owner/link-proof coverage per root. Multiple device roots can share static closures without merging root-relative contexts. |
| Launch/entry (§10) | bennett_run(program, cc_entry, args, entropy) starts device CC; it is not native main. | Publish a host call/return shim and container/root selection/feature negotiation. Define native argument/result marshalling, including ptr (whose host binding §10 does not specify), status/fault propagation, suspend/reverse/close and ownership. Do not invent a current general launch shim. |
| Budgets (§5.4; §§10–11) | Validated token tuples and identity scoped to a program/run; action_call has no primary token (§4.7). | Bind selected device invocation budgets and token equality to the launch request; charge actual device/replay work. State host/shim accounting separately; a native process has no invented global B. |
| Handles (§3.3; §10; dialect §6.6) | Register ids refer to the loaded program's ledger; run ownership and token validity are explicit. | Map host-visible opaque handles to that program/ledger with validity and cross-launch ownership checks; preserve class vectors and token identities. Do not serialize native pointers as device ids or token tuples as identities. |
| Effect tape (§7.3; §10) | Run-associated R effects and explicit copied hostcall requests; no implicit log of native host I/O. | Associate each device run and tape with the launched root and define host effect/proxy ordering and replay at the boundary, preserving §7.4 reversal. Saved-journal interchange needs its own versioned replay profile. |
| Host memory by reference (§§7.1–7.2; dialect §§8.5, 17.1, 19.4–19.5) | Device layout plus dialect snapshot/replay and boundary-address/promotion rules; copied hostcall bytes do not provide general shared pointers. | Publish the mapping and lifetime protocol named in the statement, including allocator metadata/history and pointer-rich layouts, so ordinary host memory can be supplied safely without universal host promotion. Retain existing snapshot/replay as a construction, not the sole eventual capability. |

For example native main can call marked roots A and B in distinct ISA objects.
Each has its own object/root certificate and root context []; neither
certificate inventories main or host-only libc. A shared linked device
callee is checked in each root's actual invocation tree. A native comparison
executes the corresponding materialised region/callee closure under its
recorded layout, input and tool configuration (dialect §§17.7, 19.5, 19.9).

These notes specify boundary scope and exact remaining contract work; they
implement no container, host shim or memory registration. The existing
bennett-4ht issue tracks that publication/implementation obligation. Current
device contracts and their complete estimate/proof requirements remain in
force; an unimplemented boundary is not evidence that mixed execution works.

The arithmetic is in Q[z]/(z^4+1), z=exp(i*pi/4), with rational
coefficients; H uses (z−z³)/2. There are no floating-point quantum
calculations in this check. Equality of every branch operator to V/sqrt(2)
establishes the resulting channel on all matrix units, hence also with
an external reference; the negative vectors compare density matrices.
This is exact finite mathematical verification, not a producer certificate
accepted by an implementation of the proposed contract.

```python
from fractions import Fraction as F
from pathlib import Path
from math import gcd
import hashlib
import re

def scalar(n):
    return (F(n), F(0), F(0), F(0))

ZERO, ONE = scalar(0), scalar(1)
Z = (F(0), F(1), F(0), F(0))

def add(a, b):
    return tuple(x + y for x, y in zip(a, b))

def neg(a):
    return tuple(-x for x in a)

def mul(a, b):
    out = [F(0)] * 7
    for i in range(4):
        for j in range(4):
            out[i+j] += a[i] * b[j]
    for k in range(6, 3, -1):
        out[k-4] -= out[k]
    return tuple(out[:4])

def conj(a):
    out = [a[0], F(0), F(0), F(0)]
    for k in range(1, 4):
        out[4-k] -= a[k]
    return tuple(out)

I = mul(Z, Z)
TINV = conj(Z)
HFACTOR = (F(0), F(1, 2), F(0), F(-1, 2))
assert mul(HFACTOR, HFACTOR) == scalar(F(1, 2))
assert mul(Z, TINV) == ONE
assert mul(I, I) == neg(ONE)

def compact(state):
    return {i: a for i, a in state.items() if a != ZERO}

def accumulate(state, i, a):
    state[i] = add(state.get(i, ZERO), a)

def enabled(i, controls):
    return all((i >> c) & 1 for c in controls)

def hadamard(state, wire, controls=()):
    out = {}
    mask = 1 << wire
    for i, a in state.items():
        if not enabled(i, controls):
            accumulate(out, i, a)
            continue
        a = mul(a, HFACTOR)
        low = i & ~mask

## 11. Scheduling interface (sched 0.9)

```c
typedef struct bennett_sched_request_t bennett_sched_request_t;   typedef struct bennett_sched_response_t bennett_sched_response_t;
bennett_status bennett_sched_request_new(bennett_sched_request_t** out);
bennett_status bennett_sched_request_set_object(bennett_sched_request_t*, const uint8_t*, size_t);
bennett_status bennett_sched_request_set_target(bennett_sched_request_t*, const uint8_t* jcs, size_t);
bennett_status bennett_sched_request_set_region(bennett_sched_request_t*, const char* closure_hash_hex, const char* variant, uint32_t inherited_controls);
bennett_status bennett_sched_request_set_budget(bennett_sched_request_t*, uint64_t steps, uint64_t heap, uint64_t stack, uint64_t depth);
bennett_status bennett_sched_request_set_params(bennett_sched_request_t*, const uint8_t* params_jcs, size_t);
bennett_status bennett_sched_request_set_accuracy(bennett_sched_request_t*, const char* epsilon_decimal);
bennett_status bennett_sched_request_set_capacity(bennett_sched_request_t*, const char* cls, uint64_t peak_qubits);
void           bennett_sched_request_free(bennett_sched_request_t*);
bennett_status bennett_sched_schedule(const bennett_sched_request_t*, bennett_sched_response_t** out);
typedef enum { BENNETT_SCHED_OK, BENNETT_SCHED_INFEASIBLE, BENNETT_SCHED_UNSUPPORTED } bennett_sched_outcome;
bennett_sched_outcome bennett_sched_response_outcome(const bennett_sched_response_t*);

const uint8_t* bennett_sched_response_schedule(const bennett_sched_response_t*, size_t* len);
const uint8_t* bennett_sched_response_certificate(const bennett_sched_response_t*, size_t* len);
void           bennett_sched_response_free(bennett_sched_response_t*);
```

**Generated operations and the expansion library.** Every row and
instruction expands into **generated operations** (gates, allocations,
releases). Above gate level the order is fixed by §3.6, §4.2, §4.5–§4.6
and §5.2 (dispatch comparators k = 0..n in index order; a row's
sub-operations in listed order; successor XORs; commit; cleanup). Below
that level (the gate decomposition of comparators, adders, multiplexers,
`inrange` scans and their scratch) the expansion is defined by an
**expansion library**: a versioned document, identified in the
schedule's `bindings.expansion` as `{ "name", "version", "hash" }`, that
lists for every row kind and every instruction its generated operations
in order, with their **ordinals**, scratch identities and the subset it
marks removable, and that is extensionally equal to the reference (§5.7,
T6). A consumer that does not implement the bound library rejects the
schedule (`E_SCHEDULE_INFEASIBLE`); two consumers implementing it
execute and charge the same operation set. `bennett-isa-spec` publishes
the reference library `ref` (§17 item 7). §5.7 transformations under a
schedule MUST keep the ordinals of the operations they keep and MAY
remove only operations the library marks removable.

**Schedule document** (JCS) has the following schema, including reserved
support points and optional instance/call-occurrence selectors. [deps: ISA-11-P2, ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-11.1-P9, ISA-11.1-P22, ISA-11.1-P29]

```
  { "bindings": { "closure_hash", "target_hash", "region", "variant", "inherited_controls", "budget": [B,heap,stack,depth],
                  "params_hash", "epsilon", "expansion": { "name", "version", "hash" }, "scheduler": { "name", "version" } },
    "interface_vertices"?: [ [v, …], … ],
    "entries": [ { "path": [ { "symbol": hex, "point": "<block>:<ordinal>" | "$construct" | "$step" | "$commit" | "$release", "iteration": [n, …] | "*",
                              "instance"?: [n, …] | "*", "call_index"?: n | "*" }, … ],
                   "step": n | "*",  "phase": "fwd" | "rev" | "*",  "generated": n | "*",
                   "kind": "cleanup" | "gate" | "alloc" | "start",
                   "cleanup": { "realisation": "reverse" | "recompute" },
                   "gate":    { "realisation": "native" | "<scheme name>", "epsilon_share": decimal },
                   "alloc":   { "class": "<cls>", "vertices"?: [v, …] },           /* a concrete class; "any" is not allowed in a schedule */
                   "start":   { "time": decimal } } ] }
```

**Topology placement.** With a §8.3 topology, `interface_vertices`
lists inherited control wires first as individual width-one arrays, then
one vertex array per source interface slot in §9.4 slot order; array
elements follow bit order (and region cell address then bit order). A callee inherits its actual
operand placements. `alloc.vertices`, when present, assigns every bit
of that allocation in bit order. These arrays MUST have the declared
width, resolve to the correct classes and have disjoint live uses;
without a topology they MUST be absent. Omitted placements use the
lowest-index free vertex of the resolved class, assigning bits in order;
interface slots are assigned first in slot order, then allocations in
generated order. Vertices are retained for a wire's lifetime and freed
on release; borrowed wires retain their owner's vertices. Every native
leaf, including scheme leaves, MUST satisfy §8.3 adjacency, else
`E_SCHEDULE_INFEASIBLE` naming §17 item 15. No routing is inserted.

**Matching.** Every generated operation has an **invocation chain**:
the program points of the region calls and `lift.oracle_call`s that led
to it, outermost first, ending in its own point. [deps: ISA-4.7, ISA-4.2, ISA-3.6]
`lift.call` adds no element: an lproc row in an oracle's closure generates
its expansion once per fixed row instance per step under its dispatch
predicate, independently of which `lift.call` reached that instance or
the recursion depth. [deps: ISA-3.6, ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-3.7-P6]
`path` matches
an operation iff it is a **prefix** of that chain, element by element:
`symbol` and `point` equal, and `iteration` either `*` or equal to the
vector of 0-based dynamic repetition counts of the `qloop`s enclosing
that point (outermost first; its length is the point's loop nesting
depth, `[]` outside every loop); `instance` and `call_index` match as
specified below. [deps: ISA-4.6, ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-11.1-P9]
An oracle invocation's forward step coordinate is t ∈ 1..B and its
inverse step t has `phase: rev`; the root B is `bindings.budget[0]`
and a nested oracle's B is the steps component of its own call token,
                        return err("§3.3", "gate angle must be a classical f64 argument");
                    }
                    let (controls, targets, diagonal, other) = gate_operands(i)?;
                    if f.kind == "perm_action" && (diagonal || other) {
                        return err("§5.3", "permutation action contains non-permutation gate");

                        .iter()

## 17. Decisions deferred to implementation, with defaults

Each item is one an implementer must decide to build; any decision is
conformant provided two implementations agree on the default below until
the item is revised. Items 1–5 are asserted non-semantic.

1. **Binary encoding.** Default: none; §16 text is the only interchange
   form.
2. **Gate-name registry.** Default: the names of §4.4 only; an unknown
   gate name is a load error.
3. **Further device models.** Default: the four of §7.3; an `effect` on
   any other device is `FAULT_EFFECT_UNMODELLED`.
4. **Mechanisation of §14.** Default: none; the reference interpreter is
   the oracle.
5. **Memory layout of the opaque C objects.** Default: opaque; only the
   listed functions touch them.
6. **Measurement-based cleanup** (§6.4): the normative fixup, its
   controlled variant and its certificate kind. Default: unitary cleanup
   (`reverse`); `capabilities.mbu` is `false`.
7. **Expansion library** (§11): the gate-level expansion below row
   level. Default: the library `ref` published by `bennett-isa-spec`
   (ripple-carry adders and comparators, linear `inrange` scans,
   one-hot address dispatch, clean scratch released in reverse order);
   a schedule that names no `expansion` binds `ref` at the ISA version
   in the object's header, with the full-library envelope hash published
   for that version in §11.1. [deps: ISA-9.1, ISA-11.1-P3, ISA-11.1-P4]
   For a 0.9 object this is exactly
   `{"name":"ref","version":"0.9","hash":"5de4e4995350313e50217880ba1b95de39cd043283b726337d4894efb005d197"}`,
   equally for omission and an explicit binding. [deps: ISA-11.1-P3, ISA-11.1-P4, ISA-11.1]
   §11.1 fixes its schema, ordinals, scratch identities, empty removable
   sets and deterministic decomposition algorithms. [deps: ISA-11.1]
   The bound catalog is published as `spec/ref-0.9.json`. The worked
   `ctl` action fixture (zsw.4) is in
   `conformance/fixtures/controlled-action-09/`: `controlled.bisa`,
   `target.json`, `leaves.json`, `schedule.json`, `frame.json` and
   `certificate.json`. It contains one exact controlled-X leaf, starts
   at time 0 and ends at time 1, with two live fast-class interface wires
   and no workspace. Its README records derivation and the pinned 0.8
   compatibility check separately from the 0.9 bindings.
8. **Enclosure procedure for transcendental entries** (§12.1). Default:
   Taylor series of sin and cos to the first term whose Lagrange
   remainder bound is below 2^−(`precision_bits` + 2), argument reduced
   modulo 2π with an outward-rounded enclosure of π.
9. **Default `epsilon_share`** (§8.1, §11) when a schedule assigns none.
   Default: the exact rational `bindings.epsilon / N`, where N is the
   number of gate operations in the bound-library expansion of one
   invocation of `bindings.region`, including every nested invocation,
   that have no exact native match as defined below. [deps: ISA-8.1, ISA-8.3, ISA-11-P2]
